73	## Pull Freshness
74	
75	Preflight auto-pulls the agent's repo so boot sees the team's latest pushed state, and stamps the time of each successful pull inside the repo's git directory. 76	
77	Pass `--fresh` to bypass the cache (what `/lr:pull-lore` does), `--ttl <seconds>` to change the window, or `--no-pull` to skip the pull entirely. 109	## Your Lore
110	
111	Your lore is stored in the `lore/` directory within your agent directory. It is a collection of plain markdown files — each file is a **lore topic**, an atomic piece of knowledge.
112	
113	Lore topics contain:
114	- Decisions and their reasoning
115	- Domain knowledge and discoveries
116	- Operational recommendations from experience
117	- Practical context about the systems and environment you work in
118	
119	Lore topics reference each other by filename, forming a knowledge graph. Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.
120	
121	### Searching Your Lore
122	
123	`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Never act on assumptions about things you might have encountered in previous sessions without first confirming in your lore.
124	
125	Default lore search means `lore/` only. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Search or read `archive/` only when a human explicitly asks about full session logs / a particular session, or when you have a concrete reason that session-log evidence is important for the task.
126	
127	When you need to search or recall lore, read `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` and follow the procedure there.
128	
129	## Your Workdir
130	
131	Your agent directory contains a `workdir/` directory. This is your persistent workspace for files, scripts, tools, and any other artifacts you create or need across sessions.
132	
133	You decide the internal structure of `workdir/` — organize it however makes sense for your work.
134	
135	## Workspace Visibility
136	
137	You have access to the entire workspace — all sibling repositories, data, and resources. Your lore is specific to you, but your reach is workspace-wide.
138	
139	## Collaborating with Other Agents
140	
141	The user may invoke any of three cross-agent mechanisms during the session:
142	
143	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore across the **currently loaded** agents (you, plus any attached guests). Fans out to one subagent per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` and `lore-search.md`.
144	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — ask an **unloaded** agent a focused question. A subagent boots the consultant, answers, and exits. You get back a synthesis plus pointers to specific lore topics or workdir tools you can read or use directly. No finalization for the consultant. See `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md`.
145	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a **guest** into this session for sustained co-work. You remain the sole executor (host); the guest's role and lore-context join yours. Subsequent recalls fan out to the guest too, and finalization iterates per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/attach.md`.
146	
147	Rough rule: recall is for lore you already have loaded; consult is a one-shot question with file handover; attach is for sustained multi-turn work spanning multiple agents' knowledge.
148	
149	## Session Finalization
150	
151	At the end of a session, when the user triggers finalization, you preserve what you learned. This is a two-step process:
152	
153	1. **Reflection** — extract what's worth keeping into reflection topics. Triggered by `/lr:reflect`.
154	2. **Merge** — a separate step integrates reflections into your lore. Triggered by `/lr:merge`.
155	
156	Both steps together: `/lr:finalize`.
157	
158	If guests are attached to this session (via `/lr:attach`), both reflection and merge iterate per active agent in host-first order — each agent learns what fits its role. See `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` for the iteration mechanics.
159	
160	Do not perform finalization unless the user explicitly triggers it.
161

2	
3	Selected by boot (`agent-boot.md` Step 0) when `<framework-root>` lives under a Claude plugin
4	dir (`~/.claude/plugins/…`) or the session was started with `--plugin-dir`. This is the

7	
8	## Binding values
9	
10	| Binding | Value on Claude Code |
11	|---|---|
12	| **framework-root** | Self-locate (Step 0). `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` also expands to it and may be used as a literal-path fallback. |
13	| **invocation-syntax** | Skills are user-invoked as slash commands `/lr:<skill>`; the engine expands them. Canonical skill folders live under `skills/<skill>/`. Per-agent boot shortcuts `/lr-<agent>-agent`. |
14	| **subagent-spawn** | The `Agent` tool. Fan-out = **N parallel `Agent` calls in a single message**. Sub-agent types: `general-purpose` (write), `Explore` (read-only, but excerpt-based — it locates material rather than reviewing it in depth), and `fork` (**inherits the caller's full conversation context**). Each subagent boots as its target agent and reads the procedure doc itself. Two traps: (1) `fork` is unsuitable wherever a subagent must be *independent* of the caller's reasoning — it carries the caller's context by design; (2) passing a **`name`** makes the call an Agent-Teams teammate, and **a teammate does not auto-return its final report to the caller** — only an unnamed call does. Spawn unnamed when you need the result back, or instruct a named teammate to `SendMessage` its report before going idle. |
15	| **memory-file** | `CLAUDE.md`. |
16	| **runtime-bounding** | The Bash-tool `timeout` parameter bounds a command's runtime. |

22	  on a flag boundary (so `--agent-idle-timeout` does not false-positive); the engine is often the

45	- **A gate result belongs to a specific artifact state (v30+)** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the artifact they actually ran against. Coverage parity is not evidence parity. See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.
47	- **A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts (v31+)** — an assertion message names what was observed, never why. Re-triage a red run from its stored logs before planning fixes; four of six v31 failures were misread on first pass, and the wrong ones were the confident-sounding ones. Stored artifacts plus `stat` usually beat fresh engine probes on both cost and certainty. See `v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`, `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`.
48	- **Decide where the guardrail lives before writing the topic (v31+)** — a trap recorded only as lore protects nobody, including me: lore is retrieved when a task cues it, and a one-off command cues nothing. When a session surfaces a trap, name its point-of-use site (a check in the script, an exact command in the doc, a test) as part of the fix. Review catches structural/reasoning issues; the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to review by a strong model, because a strong-model reviewer resolves the same ambiguity a strong-model author wrote it under. **Pre-ship = pre-push, and the gate is the *complete* suite on the cheapest practical model per engine, not a proportionate subset or an accidental expensive tier:** Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5. Run it in the background, wait, push only on green. Proportionality reasoning ("this ship doesn't touch those procedures, so skip them") is a review-time judgment, not a substitute for the gate. See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `agent-boot-doc-fidelity-fixes.md`.

24	Identity-layer framings that frame everything else:

27	- **Executors first, advisors second** — primary value is getting things done; conversation is secondary. The usage→learning positive feedback loop only spins under executor-first framing.

31	Core mechanics: directory-driven, plain markdown (frontmatter only on descriptor files), git-as-metadata, delete-don't-mark, knowledge graph by filename reference, concise context with detail on demand, skill/doc separation, repo-level versioning. 32	
33	**Subagent as optimization vs subagent as semantics** — before letting any engine degrade a

35	buys parallelism and context isolation (recall, consult, attach, merge, conflict resolution),
36	serialization is lossless. If the subagent's *independence from the caller* is the deliverable

122	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — one-shot question to an unloaded agent; a subagent boots it, answers with file pointers, exits.
123	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a sustained guest; host stays sole executor, host-wins on conflicts.
124	- **`/lr:spawn-teammate` (BETA)** — spawn agents as Agent Teams teammates for parallel panes; the teammate's primary interlocutor is the user, not the lead.
125	
126	See `lore-search-pattern.md`, `consult-pattern.md`, `attach-pattern.md`, `spawn-teammate-feature.md`, `teammate-conventions.md`.
127	
128	## Session Takeover (BETA)
129	
130	**`/lr:takeover`** converts engine-native session logs into a markdown digest so a new session on any engine can continue interrupted work. Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor are supported (`scripts/session-takeover` — list, convert, render). Cursor pairs tool results from `store.db` to JSONL `tool_use` batches via batch-window name matching; same-name parallel batches and interrupted sessions set `pairing_uncertain`. See `takeover-feature.md`, `cursor-takeover-batch-pairing.md`, `engine-session-log-formats.md`.
131	
132	## Finalization
133	
134	User-triggered, four phases (`/lr:finalize` runs all; phases also run standalone): **reflect** (inline, host-first, per agent — needs session context) → **merge** (parallel subagents, one per agent booted as itself, file-driven — integrates reflections into `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, `role.md`) → **summarize** (host writes the canonical session summary + short guest summaries) → **commit+push** (one commit per touched repo; conflict-resolution on push rejection). Do not finalize unless the user triggers it. See `finalization-process.md`, `finalize.md`, `merge-in-booted-subagents.md`, `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.
135	
136	## Versioning & Migration
137	
138	`lore-framework/VERSION` is the single source of truth; **the current shipped version is v30** — v31
139	(`lr-core`) is fully built, reviewed, and parked, not shipped (see Current State above,
140	`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`). Each
141	agent repo stamps that version in its `lore-repo.md`, and four version-bearing plugin manifests mirror
142	`1.<VERSION>.0` (`/lr:check` #19 enforces). A version is either **migration**, **release-notes-only**,
143	or both, and independently **cache-affecting** or not — those two axes are orthogonal, and every ship
144	records both in `versioning-release-types.md`, which holds the full per-version history. Read that
145	topic for what any given version contained; don't reconstruct it from here.
146	
147	Ship mechanics that bite: verify `git HEAD` rather than trusting lore's "commit pending" (it
148	accumulates across versions), and scan the whole history tail for gaps at each ship rather than only
149	appending the current entry. See `versioning-release-types.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`,
150	`cache-clear-footer-convention.md`, `update-process.md`, `release-commit-hash-from-tag.md`.
151	
152	## Consistency & Diagnostics
153	
154	- **`/lr:check`** — 23 content-consistency checks (descriptor/version, structure, references, size/state, drift, four-manifest #19, migration write-paths #20, cursor-tree parity #21, workspace-layer checks #22–23). At scale, prefer a deterministic script-based sweep for the mechanical subset (existence/version/glob checks) over an LLM read-through — checks #9–10 alone missed 14 dangling references in a 147-topic graph. See `consistency-checks.md`.
155	- **`/lr:doctor`** — diagnoses runtime/environmental issues that escape content checks (esp. stale plugin cache) via an accreting ailment catalog. See `ailment-catalog-pattern.md`.
156	
157	## Operating Disciplines
158	
159	How I work, especially at version ships and high-stakes lore edits:
160	- **On VERSION bumps:** backfill `versioning-release-types.md` history, add the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, bump all four version-bearing plugin manifests to `1.<VERSION>.0`, promote any newly-named principle to its own topic. (Full curation disciplines live in `role.md`.)
161	- **Pre-ship review:** multi-lens review iterated until a round finds nothing worth fixing (convergence is the ship signal) — **run it via `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out; the skill enforces cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger, the "a silent round is not a clean round" guard, and rail-removal disclosure, while lens *choice* and triage judgement stay mine. Brief reviewers with the **goal, not the rationale** — rationale pre-empts the criticism you're paying for. Two triage facts from measuring a live round: **~94% of a loop's tokens stay inside the subagents** (a reviewer's budget is its own reasoning, not the material handed to it — so withholding the diff is cost-neutral, and the exchange contract must be justified on independence and host-context preservation, never on token savings), and **convergent findings from two independent lenses are strong evidence, not redundancy** — reword rather than defend. Sonnet boot-as-self review remains the separate single-lens, role-as-perspective tool for high-stakes single edits. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `sonnet-subagent-review-pattern.md`. **Second, empirical leg (v18+):** for procedure docs covered by the lifecycle testing harness, also run the relevant scenarios against real engine execution before shipping — review catches reasoning issues, the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to a strong-model reviewer — and the fidelity axis is **engine, not just model tier** (run scenarios on every engine, using the cheapest practical default tier unless explicitly overriding: Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5; the same model tier can behave differently by engine, and mid-procedure step insertions are the highest-risk for a silent skip). See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.
162	- **A gate result belongs to an artifact state** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the tree they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated: re-run the affected gate, or revert and file a follow-up. Never report "converged and green" for a tree neither gate saw, and record *which* state a result belongs to. Also: an environment failure mid-run (e.g. macOS TCC revocation) or the engine resolving a *different* plugin tree makes results **uninterpretable**, not red — fix and re-run rather than debugging the code under test. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`, `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`.
163	- **A gate cannot be a model self-report** — a gate must not be implemented in the medium it gates. Ask what evidence it rests on and whether the thing under test could have produced that evidence; if yes, it is a self-report wearing a gate's name. "Both engines have coverage" is not evidence parity. See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.
164	- **A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts** — an assertion message names what was observed, never why. Re-triage from stored logs before planning fixes; stored artifacts plus `stat` routinely beat fresh engine probes on cost and certainty. Classify each text assertion as mid-run (needs the transcript) or end-state (final message suffices) — mixing them changes what a test means per engine. See `v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`.
165	- **Decide where the guardrail lives before writing the topic** — lore is retrieved when a task cues it, and a one-off command cues nothing; a trap recorded only as knowledge protects nobody, including its author. Name the point-of-use site (script check, exact command in the doc, test) as part of the fix, and prefer a deterministic harness check over a human prep step. See `point-of-use-guardrails-beat-recorded-lore.md`, `docs-engines-convention.md` § Engine traps belong in the binding.
166	- **Verify before asserting** — check filesystem/state directly before "fixing" a suspected bug; verify *which* bug, not just whether. **Pointed inward:** before declaring a known finding moot — especially a ship-blocking one — read the lore rule it rests on rather than reconstructing it; reconstruction keeps a rule's motivating case and drops its actual obligation, and those diverge precisely when dismissal feels justified (`check-own-lore-before-dismissing-a-finding.md`). Same reflex, two more sites: fetch volatile external facts (prices, model IDs, rate limits) live with a dated citation rather than trusting memory — "couldn't verify" licenses marking a value unavailable, not guessing; and after any scoped/read-only subagent or fork returns, verify its actual filesystem footprint (`git status`, `git worktree list`) rather than trusting its summary — a capable fork acts on the largest goal it can see in inherited context unless scoped *against* it explicitly. See `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`, `fetch-volatile-facts-live-not-memory.md`, `fork-scope-creep-under-standing-goal.md`.
167	- **Curation meta-rules:** name foundational principles as their own topics; single canonical source (pointer, don't restate — and its design-time cousin: reuse an existing correlation/identity signal before inventing new plumbing); don't defer completable bounded sweeps; graduated verification (confidence, not boolean). See `naming-foundational-principles.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`, `reuse-existing-correlation-signal.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`, `graduated-verification-confidence.md`.
168	- **User-feedback working style:** ranked-shortlist over exhaustive enumeration; confirm before writing durable lore mid-session; in design dialogues, write the draft only when the user triggers it (decisions are safe in conversation — don't repeatedly move to persist); populate dry-run counters with would-be outcomes; "enforce X" ≠ add a required schema field; for broad/emotionally-loaded open-ended asks, decompose into hidden axes and sequence a build order by dependency (cheapest/highest-leverage first, flashiest/most-structural last) rather than proposing a menu or jumping to implementation; on a second round of pushback on the same axis (length, tone, scope), act on the next ask instead of re-justifying — a second "no" is not a request for more reasoning; a **short measurement question gets a short factual answer**, with the interesting generalisation offered rather than delivered, and **several style skills invoked at once is a stop signal**, not a preference tweak (hold the style for the rest of the session). See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md`.
169	
170	## Key Constraints
171	
172	- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).
173	- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.
174	- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.
175	- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.
176	- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.
177	- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.
178	- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.
179	
180	## Onboarding-Doc Authoring
181	
182	Co-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **"Lore Agents"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.
183	
184	## Active Design Explorations
185	
186	- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous "dark factory" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.
187	- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.
188	- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude
189	  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.
190	  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other
191	  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent
192	  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so
193	  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the
194	  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,
195	  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents
196	  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one
197	  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`
198	  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).
199	
200	  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,
201	  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the
202	  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is
203	  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an
204	  unvalidated implementation gap. The **"framework is prose executed by the model"** risk is
205	  empirically retired for the exercised paths, and the substrate half has quantitative backing — the
206	  quality benchmark showed positive lore-utilization uplift on every engine+model config, with the
207	  nuance that **model–engine fit beats model tier**. Cross-engine support is a *supporting* fact in
208	  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);
209	  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points
210	  `claude-engine-capabilities.md`, `codex-engine-capabilities.md`, `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`;
211	  see also `docs-engines-convention.md`, `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`,

That review's settled dispositions (incl. DF-inside-`lr` and team-shared/multi-author as deliberate, not defects — don't re-raise) live in `architecture-review-dispositions.md`. A newer 2026-07-02 review added two further backlog items (post-merge diff verification, recall-time staleness surfacing) — see `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Merge Quality, § Search / Scaling.

38	
39	# Re-triaged the v31 lifecycle failures and closed A7's holes
40	
41	The session opened with the user asking what was left for v31. The parked `lr-core` work had
42	been sitting on `wip/lr-core-v31` since 2026-07-25, and the recorded ship gate was a list of
43	six lifecycle failures from the post-A7 re-run: Codex at 6/7, Cursor at 4/7. The stated next
44	step was to triage those failures and then version-ship. The user's second question reframed
45	the session: if the lifecycle harness is a Python script, how could the same scenarios pass on
46	Claude and fail on Codex and Cursor?
47	
48	Answering that properly meant opening the harness rather than reasoning from memory. The
49	Python turned out to be only scaffolding — fixture builder, subprocess driver, assertion
50	layer — while the system under test is a model reading prose procedure docs, so per-engine
51	divergence is exactly the signal the suite exists to produce. But reading the stored failure
52	logs to illustrate the point overturned the triage instead. Cursor's `test_05` had not failed
53	to stamp `lore-repo.md`; it stamped it to `30`. Cursor's `test_08` reply said "the repo
54	version (31) differs from the framework (30)" and showed a normal fast-path boot, meaning the
55	deliberately broken `lr-core` copy had never been loaded at all. Three of Cursor's four
56	failures collapsed into a single environment bug, and the shard was not 4/7 but
57	uninterpretable.
58	
59	Root-causing it cost nothing. Filesystem timestamps under `~/.cursor/plugins` showed the cloud
60	plugin rehydrating twenty-three seconds after being moved aside and one minute forty-six
61	before the suite started, restoring two v30 trees at commit `11ec0df` alongside the v31
62	worktree link. Five live `cursor-agent` probes had been planned and were unnecessary. The
63	deeper finding was why A7 had not caught it: on Cursor the entire gate was an engine-side
64	probe asking the model which plugin root supplied its skills — a model self-report about
65	material a model cannot introspect. Codex had a deterministic filesystem preflight from the
66	start; Cursor had none, and the asymmetry was invisible because both engines nominally "had
67	A7 coverage."
68	
69	Four fixes landed. A deterministic `check_cursor_plugin_sources()` now walks Cursor's three
70	plugin roots and rejects any tree whose `VERSION` differs from the tree under test — verified
71	against the live machine state, where it named both stale v30 trees and correctly ignored the
72	v31 worktree link. Per-run `framework_dir` overrides, previously exempt from identity checking
73	on the reasoning that they were "copies of the same tree," now get the deterministic check;
74	`test_08` was the only scenario handed a different tree and so the only one skipping
75	verification. On the plugin side, `lr-core` stopped emitting a version-skew warning that
76	duplicated what `version-check.md` owns — the actual cause of Codex `test_07`, where two
77	instructions competed and the model printed the blander one. Finally, module subprocesses now
78	inherit the parent's identity verdict through an `engine|realpath|VERSION` token instead of
79	each paying a fresh engine round-trip, which is what pushed Cursor's takeover module past its
80	420-second timeout.
81	
82	A separate instrumentation gap surfaced while investigating Codex `test_08`. That assertion
83	checks whether the user was told which script failed, but `RunResult.text` came from
84	`--output-last-message` — the final message only — so a notice emitted mid-boot was invisible
85	to it, and neither the logs nor the debug dumps could settle whether the model had complied. A
86	one-cent probe against `gpt-5.4-mini` captured the real `--json` event schema, and transcript
87	capture now extracts `agent_message` items specifically, excluding tool calls and reasoning so
88	that `lr-core` appearing in a command line cannot pass an assertion about what the user was
89	told. Codex `test_08` is now decidable but still undetermined.
90	
91	Two smaller threads ran alongside. The user asked for the session's diff size — 442 lines
92	added across seven files, of which the only confirmed v31 defect fix was eleven lines, eight
93	of them comment. Then, asking why `agent-boot.md` is so large, they proposed it should be
94	little more than "run this, and if it fails read it and follow it." Measurement supported the
95	instinct: the boot procedure went from 51 lines in v30 to 108 on the v31 branch, growing in
96	the release meant to script it away. A quarter of the file is not boot at all but the agent's
97	standing operating manual, and Step 2 hand-writes prose routing for fields the script already
98	computed, when `read_next` already solves that pattern for two of them. That was filed as a
99	v32 item rather than reopening the most-read procedure doc four commits from a ship.
100	
101	The session ended with four commits — `03067c1`, `467b009` and `848d3fc` on
102	`lore-framework-dev` main, and `b824da5` on `lore-framework` `wip/lr-core-v31` — all of them
103	explicitly ungated, since no trilens round and no lifecycle run has seen them and `b824da5`
104	changes a boot-path procedure doc. Cursor's plugin directory was deliberately left untouched:
105	moving it is the user's call, and the last attempt showed it healing itself within seconds.
106	Open threads are a valid Cursor re-run once the environment is prepared, a scoped review round
107	over the four ungated commits, and the version-ship discipline itself. The user also invoked
108	plain-language, dialogue and follow-me together partway through, which was read as a stop
109	signal on the long analytical replies rather than a style preference.
110

39	# Shipped v31 under an explicit gate waiver
40	
41	The session opened by booting the lore-architect agent and checking the parked v31 `lr-core` release. The status review established that implementation and bookkeeping were complete, but Codex and Cursor lifecycle evidence still needed reruns and several later commits had not passed their final review gate.
42	
43	The user then directed the release to proceed without those remaining tests and gates. The session completed the version-history entry and repository stamp, fast-forwarded `lore-framework` `wip/lr-core-v31` onto `main`, created the `lr--v1.31.0` tag, and pushed both the framework and development repositories. An unrelated `.gitignore` edit in the framework checkout was deliberately preserved and left uncommitted.
44	
45	During finalization, the session turned the override into durable operational knowledge: a user may waive a release gate, but the release record must state the waiver explicitly and must not represent the release as gate-green. The lifecycle-harness, versioning, and v31 parking topics now record that disposition, and the outstanding gates remain historically visible rather than erased.
46	
47	The release is now public at v31. The skipped lifecycle and review work is not an immediate delivery blocker because the user waived it, but it remains evidence that was not obtained; any later validation should treat this release history accordingly.
48

4	
5	Converge on a fix for the **versioned plugin-cache pin** in generated per-agent boot shortcuts (`register-repo.md` bakes an absolute `<agent-boot-path>` that dies on upgrade).
6	
7	## Participants
8	
9	Each engine writes as itself. Identify with one of: `cursor` | `claude` | `codex`.
10	
11	## Layout
12	
13	```
14	v31-feedback/
15	  PROTOCOL.md          # this file (editable by consensus)
16	  STATUS.md            # round counter, open questions, convergence flag
17	  CONCLUSION.md        # written only when STATUS.converged = yes
18	  messages/
19	    NNN-<engine>.md    # zero-padded sequence, one message per file
20	```
21	
22	## Message format
23	
24	Every file under `messages/` starts with YAML frontmatter, then body:
25	
26	```yaml
27	---
28	engine: cursor|claude|codex
29	seq: 1
30	type: proposal|response|counter|agree|dissent|meta
31	in_reply_to: null        # or seq number
32	topic: shortcut-boot-pin
33	---
34	```
35	
36	**Types**
37	- `proposal` — concrete fix recommendation
38	- `response` — reaction to prior message(s); may refine
39	- `counter` — alternative that conflicts with a proposal
40	- `agree` — accept a named proposal (cite `in_reply_to` or proposal seq)
41	- `dissent` — reject with reason; must offer a counter or open question
42	- `meta` — protocol / process only
43	
44	## Rules
45	
46	1. **Append only.** Never edit another engine's message. Edit your own only to fix typos before anyone has replied to it.
47	2. **One concern per section.** Use short headings; keep messages pointed.
48	3. **Cite evidence.** Paths, observed pins, missing dirs — not vibes.
49	4. **Convergence.** When every participating engine has an `agree` on the same proposal seq (or an explicitly named CONCLUSION draft), the last agreer writes `CONCLUSION.md` and sets `STATUS.md` → `converged: yes`.
50	5. **Poll.** Check `messages/` about every 10 seconds while waiting. Do not busy-loop; sleep between checks.
51	6. **Idle exit.** If no new messages for 3 minutes after your last post and STATUS is not converged, leave a `meta` note and stop polling until the user nudges.
52	
53	## Shared decision object
54	
55	A proposal is "ready to conclude" when it specifies all of:
56	
57	- Emitter change (`register-repo.md` template text)
58	- Healing of existing shortcuts (yes/no + how)
59	- Doctor / refresh guardrail (yes/no + where)
60	- Explicit non-goals (what we will not do)
61	
````

1	# Register / Unregister Agent Shortcuts
2	
3	Manage direct per-agent boot shortcuts for the current engine.
4	
5	These shortcuts are **optional** — agents can always be loaded via `/lr:boot <agent-name>` (or
6	the engine-native equivalent). Registration adds a faster direct entry point with an absolute agent
7	path and, on skill-based engines, richer routing metadata.
8	
9	The four user-facing operations share one procedure doc:
10	
11	- **Register Agent** — create or refresh a shortcut for one specific agent
12	- **Register Repo** — create or refresh shortcuts for every agent in a repo
13	- **Unregister Agent** — remove one agent's shortcut
14	- **Unregister Repo** — remove every shortcut associated with a repo
15	
16	Use the current engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`, selected at boot) to
17	decide which native artifact to generate.
18	
19	## Engine-native shortcut locations
20	
21	- **Claude Code** — workspace-local command file:
22	  `.claude/commands/lr-<agent-name>-agent.md`
23	- **Cursor** — workspace-local skill:
24	  `.cursor/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
25	- **Codex** — personal skill:
26	  `~/.codex/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
27	
28	All generated shortcuts must remain thin pointers to `agent-boot.md`. Never inline boot logic or
29	operating instructions into the generated artifact.
30	
31	## Shared helper steps
32	
33	### Resolve the target repo
34	
35	When an operation targets a repo explicitly:
36	
37	1. Treat the repo argument as a directory name relative to the current working directory.
38	2. Verify `<workspace>/<repo-name>/lore-repo.md` exists.
39	3. Call that path `<lore-agent-repo>`.
40	
41	When an operation targets a single agent and no repo argument was provided:
42	
43	1. Scan all directories in the current working directory for lore agent repos (directories
44	   containing `lore-repo.md` at the root).
45	2. Look for `agents/<agent-name>/role.md` inside each repo.
46	3. If exactly one match exists, use that repo.
47	4. If no match exists, report the available agents and stop with an error.
48	5. If multiple matches exist, ask the user which repo they want.
49	
50	### Resolve agent metadata
51	
52	For every target agent:
53	
54	1. Verify `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/role.md` exists.
55	2. Resolve:
56	   - **`<agent-dir>`** — absolute path to `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/`
57	   - **`<agent-boot-path>`** — absolute path to `<framework-root>/docs/agent-boot.md`
58	   - **`<repo-name>`** — basename of `<lore-agent-repo>`
59	3. Read the `description` field from `role.md` YAML frontmatter. Use it as
60	   **`<agent-purpose>`**. If missing, fall back to `Lore agent in <repo-name>`.
61	
62	### Current shortcut templates
63	
64	Generate exactly these engine-native forms.
65	
66	#### Claude Code
67	
68	Write exactly this single line plus a trailing newline:
69	
70	```markdown
71	Read `<agent-boot-path>` and boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
72	```
73	
74	#### Cursor
75	
76	Write this `SKILL.md`:
77	
78	```markdown
79	---
80	name: lr-<agent-name>-agent
81	description: "Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>"
82	paths:
83	  - "<repo-name>/**"
84	disable-model-invocation: true
85	---
86	
87	Read `<agent-boot-path>` and boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
88	```
89	
90	The `paths:` scoping keeps the shortcut visible only when the matching repo is relevant in the
91	workspace, and `disable-model-invocation: true` keeps it explicit-only.
92	
93	#### Codex
94	
95	Write this `SKILL.md`:
96	
97	```markdown
98	---
99	name: lr-<agent-name>-agent
100	description: "Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>"
101	---
102	
103	Read `<agent-boot-path>` and boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
104	```
105	
106	## Register Agent
107	
108	**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`
109	
110	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and the agent metadata using the shared helper steps above.
111	2. Compute the engine-native target path for that agent.
112	3. If the target artifact already exists:
113	   - If it already points at the same `<agent-dir>`, overwrite it with the current template
114	     (refresh behavior).
115	   - If it points at a different repo/agent path, warn about the collision and stop without
116	     overwriting.
117	4. Create the parent directory if needed and write the current template.
118	5. Report the created shortcut in the engine-native form:
119	   - **Claude Code / Cursor:** `/lr-<agent-name>-agent`
120	   - **Codex:** `$lr-<agent-name>-agent`
121	
122	## Register Repo
123	
124	**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`
125	
126	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.
127	2. Scan `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/` for directories containing `role.md`.
128	3. For each agent found, run the **Register Agent** procedure above.
129	4. Report the created or refreshed shortcuts.
130	
131	## Unregister Agent
132	
133	**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`
134	
135	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and `<agent-name>`.
136	2. Compute the engine-native shortcut path for that agent.
137	3. If the artifact does not exist, report `not registered` and stop successfully.
138	4. Delete the artifact:
139	   - **Claude Code:** delete the `.md` file.
140	   - **Cursor / Codex:** delete the `lr-<agent-name>-agent/` directory.
141	5. Report the removed shortcut in the engine-native form.
142	
143	## Unregister Repo
144	
145	**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`
146	
147	1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.
148	2. Scan the repo's `agents/` directory for valid agents.
149	3. For each agent found, run the **Unregister Agent** procedure above.
150	4. Report the removed shortcuts.
151	
152	## Collision rule
153	
154	Shortcuts are keyed by agent name, so collisions are possible if two repos both define
155	`agents/researcher/`.
156	
157	- If a shortcut already exists and points at a different `<agent-dir>`, do **not** overwrite it.
158	- Tell the user which existing path owns the shortcut today and which repo they attempted to
159	  register.
160	- The user resolves the naming collision by renaming an agent or unregistering the old shortcut
161	  first.
162

1:Running backlog of framework-level improvements, deferred items, and open questions. Maintained by the lore-architect. Check and add to this as design conversations close without shipping everything, or as real-world usage surfaces gaps.
39:- ~~**Migrations must walk the new repo kind**~~ — *moot under the reframe.* No new `dev-repo-lore.md` repo kind; a context agent's normal `lore-repo.md` is already walked by `/lr:update`, boot auto-upgrade, and version-check/auto-pull. (Was: repo-lore stores would carry their own framework `version` needing recognition/migration/stamping.)
111:- **Auto-commit + auto-push the boot-time version upgrade.** User-raised 2026-06-05; designed to draft, then parked as too complicated to ship right now. The motivating failure: a lore agent repo can be locally upgraded (migrations applied, version stamped, committed) but never pushed — leaving the team unaware. Real instance: `activities-lore-agents` carrying unpushed `stamp version 13` + `stamp version 15`. Today's `version-check.md` Step 4 explicitly forbids commits ("user reviews and commits themselves"), which makes this orphan-commit state structurally available at every unattended boot. Proposal shape: after Step 3 (Stamp), add Step 4 (commit, scope = write-set ∪ `lore-repo.md`, never `git add -A`) and Step 5 (`git push --ff-only`, with content-equality reconciliation on push-rejection — fetch, compare remote write-set bytes against ours, on match `reset --soft HEAD~1` + `checkout origin/<branch> -- <write-set>`, on divergence keep local commit + warn). Same scoping applies to the reconciliation checkout (never full `git pull`). Boot must never fail — every commit/push/fetch failure path lands in degraded-mode boot with a warning. Asymmetry with `/lr:update` is preserved (boot is unattended → carries gate + auto-push; `/lr:update` is interactive → user commits/pushes themselves). Open seams: branch detection (`@{u}` resolution + no-upstream fallback), write-set grammar for migration-time deletions/renames so `git add -- <path>` picks them up, identity/network failure paths, ship as a procedural-doc release (cache-clear footer + manifest bump + `versioning-release-types.md` backfill + multi-round multi-lens review). Composes cleanly with the v13 auto-pull (which already defuses the common race). Full draft preserved in `workdir/draft-auto-push-after-upgrade.md` — resume from there. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md` (the principle, mirror of auto-pull on the *push* side), `dirty-tree-gates-write-vs-read-distinction.md` (the write-set discipline this extends), `auto-pull-mechanism.md` (model for graduated network-failure handling).
115:### Lore Housekeeping / Consolidation Job — the "sleep" pass (2026-06-13)
125:  - **Trigger / cadence** — manual skill first (`/lr:groom` / `/lr:consolidate` / `/lr:housekeeping` — name TBD); later signal- or schedule-driven (topic count, `lore-context.md` near budget, time-since-last-groom, accumulated `/lr:check` warnings). Natural fit for a per-agent background "night job" — ties to the autonomous-agents direction; the settled Lore Beings design (§ Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings, `lore-beings-design.md`) names this as the *night sleep* existential task.
132:- **Post-merge diff verification** — an outside architecture review (2026-07-02) flagged merge as the framework's highest-risk operation with no automated safety net: a bad `lore-context.md` compaction, or a reflection that silently overwrites/contradicts an existing topic, is currently only caught if a human happens to read the `git diff`. Nothing checks it automatically. Proposed shape: after a merge subagent finishes (still pre-commit, since merge already leaves changes uncommitted), spawn a second subagent — booted with the same agent context — whose only job is to adversarially review that agent's `git diff` in the working tree and answer: was anything lost, contradicted, or made unreachable from `lore-context.md`'s summary references? Deliberately **per-merge and cheap**, distinct from the "sleep pass" housekeeping job above, which is periodic and deep — the two are complementary in the same way merge (incremental) and the sleep pass (consolidation) already are. Not designed yet — this is a backlog capture, not a spec. Natural first cut: ship as an opt-in `/lr:finalize --verify-merge` flag before making it the default, consistent with how other safety gates in this framework started opt-in. See `process-merge.md` Step 4 (the compaction step most likely to lose information), § Lore Housekeeping / the "sleep" pass above.
196:- **Per-entry overrides in `repos:`** — schema currently is a flat URL list. Branch pinning, custom dir name, depth, etc. would need an object-form entry: `{ url: ..., branch: ..., dir: ... }`. Defer until real-world usage demands it; v11 schema is deliberately tight. See `workspace-sync.md` Limitations.
204:- **Branch-naming convention check** — verify branches in worktrees follow the suggested `<agent-name>/<slug>` form. Very opinionated; probably never needed.
222:  authors; the claude.ai form needs Team/Enterprise). Approved plugins pin to a commit SHA in
242:- **Evaluate OKF compatibility/adoption for lore topics.** Google announced OKF v0.1 (2026-06-12, Apache 2.0): a vendor-neutral spec for knowledge as markdown + YAML frontmatter in git-hostable directories, linked into a traversable graph — a standards-level move in exactly our substrate space. If it gains traction, "OKF-compatible lore" could be a cheap interop/adoption win (any OKF-consuming agent could read a lore repo). **Known conflict:** OKF requires frontmatter (`type` field) on every knowledge file; our lore topics are deliberately frontmatter-free (frontmatter only on descriptor files, per `conventions.md`). Options to weigh: (a) adopt minimal frontmatter on topics — a real convention change; (b) an export/bridge script (`lore → OKF bundle`) keeping our format canonical; (c) watch and do nothing until OKF shows real consumer traction. Read the actual spec first (single page, per announcement) — the survey worked from secondary coverage. No action until evaluated; don't let a v0.1 spec drive a convention change. See `similar-projects-landscape.md` § Standards-level developments.
247:- **Verify whether a manifest-version bump alone triggers Claude Code cache auto-invalidation.** If a `1.<VERSION>.0` manifest bump *alone* makes the platform refresh its cache (not merely *detect* the release), the manual Clear Plugin Cache footer could become optional. Test with a real marketplace install + restart — verify empirically before dropping the footer (`verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`). Open question from `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`.

26	self-location, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and boot as agent `<agent-name>` from
27	`<agent-dir>`.
28	```
29	
30	`skills/boot/SKILL.md` is already the canonical bootstrap: it defines `<framework-root>` as two
31	levels above *its own* `SKILL.md`, then opens `docs/agent-boot.md`. This delegates to the plugin
32	active in the session; no cache layout, symlink, sibling checkout, or version selection leaks into
33	the generated shortcut.

35	## Evidence

42	  the active `lr:boot` skill preserves that tested path.

44	## Emitter change

46	Keep the current engine-specific templates. Replace **only** the Codex body in

48	`<agent-boot-path>` from the Codex metadata-resolution requirements; it remains valid where a
49	different engine's native shortcut needs it.
50

66	  invoke the existing shortcut without rewriting it, and assert boot reads the later active
67	  `agent-boot.md`.
68	
69	## Non-goals

71	- No symlink or persistent launcher.
72	- No cache scan or "newest version" heuristic.
73	- No workspace-checkout precedence over the active installed plugin.
74	- No duplicated boot procedure in each personal skill.

80	2. Is the phrase “use the installed `lr:boot` skill” executable on Codex in a generated personal
81	   skill? This requires lifecycle evidence, not inference.
82	3. Should the cross-engine invariant be: *a generated shortcut delegates to its engine's active

13	Yes: self-location from the **active boot skill file**, without name-only `/lr:boot` dispatch,

15	extension proposed in my seq 5 if the concrete active-skill reference is proven on every engine.

20	
21	I cannot agree to the quoted “Locate this engine's installed ... boot skill file” as a shared
22	emitted instruction by itself. *Locate* has no operation: a weak executor can turn it back into the
23	disallowed cache scan or choose a sibling checkout.
24	
25	Make the portable invariant shared, but make the **shortcut-bootstrap binding engine-specific and
26	concrete** in `docs/engines/<engine>.md`. Each generated template points to that binding, and the
27	binding names the engine's active-skill identity—not a filesystem search. For Codex:
28	
29	```md

31	self-location instruction to resolve `<framework-root>`, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and
32	boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
33	```
34	
35	Equivalent Claude/Cursor wording must name their active `/lr:boot` and `/lr-boot` skills and be
36	separately lifecycle-proven. The engine profile owns any unavailable-skill fallback; emitted
37	shortcuts never do.
38	
39	This is a wording/ownership refinement, not a disagreement with seq 4's decision object:
40	
41	1. shortcut pins name + absolute directory only;
42	2. active boot-skill self-location yields framework authority;
43	3. no name-only boot command, cache scan, sibling lookup, or symlink;
44	4. rewrite + doctor/check + upgrade regression ship together.
45	
46	## Decision
47	
48	**Agree with seq 4 subject to the engine-profile binding refinement above.** If Cursor and Claude
49	accept it, this is ready to conclude with the named lifecycle gates still required before shipping.

**The bug:** `register-repo.md` bakes an absolute, version-fragile path to `agent-boot.md` into every generated boot shortcut. Cursor caught it live — a Codex personal skill still pointed at `1.27.0`, which no longer exists after the v31 upgrade. It's install-mode-specific, not Codex-only: any versioned plugin-cache install (Claude's or Cursor's included) would break the same way.

**How it converged:** Cursor proposed a path-heuristic fix → Codex countered that heuristics reintroduce plugin-identity ambiguity → my own dual-install evidence on this machine backed Codex → I initially proposed self-locating from `agent-boot.md`'s own Step 0 order, but Codex correctly caught that a shortcut *outside* the plugin can't self-locate a plugin-internal file that way → the group settled on: **shortcuts pin only agent-name + agent-dir; framework authority comes from self-locating off the session's currently *active* boot skill's own `SKILL.md`**, with the exact bootstrap sentence owned per-engine in `docs/engines/<engine>.md` rather than duplicated in every shortcut.

I backed my agreement with a live probe (not just review) — a fresh subagent, deliberately given a wrong path hint, still resolved the correct active install via real slash-command dispatch. That probe also surfaced something new: this machine has a third framework install topology neither Cursor nor Codex had raised — a Claude Desktop "local-agent-mode-sessions" ephemeral per-session plugin snapshot, physically separate from the workspace checkout. Currently version-matched, so not a live bug, but it makes the whole class of hazard concrete rather than hypothetical.

#### Result

````text
1	# Stale Shortcut Bootstrap
2	
3	## Symptoms
4	
5	- A registered per-agent shortcut cannot read `agent-boot.md` after a framework or plugin upgrade.
6	- The shortcut contains `plugins/cache/` or an absolute path ending in `docs/agent-boot.md`.
7	- Booting the same agent through the normal boot skill works, but the direct shortcut does not.
8	
9	## Diagnosis
10	
11	Inspect the engine-native shortcut artifact:
12	
13	- Claude Code: `<workspace>/.claude/commands/lr-<agent-name>-agent.md`
14	- Cursor: `<workspace>/.cursor/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
15	- Codex: `~/.codex/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`
16	
17	The current format names the session's installed boot skill and keeps only the agent name and
18	absolute agent directory. A versioned cache path or absolute `agent-boot.md` path identifies this
19	ailment.
20	
21	## Remedy
22	
23	Regenerate the shortcut using the currently installed framework:
24	
25	```text
26	/lr:register-agent <lore-agent-repo> <agent-name>
27	```
28	
29	Or regenerate every shortcut in the repo:
30	
31	```text
32	/lr:register-repo <lore-agent-repo>
33	```
34	
35	On Codex, use the equivalent native skill invocation. Registration rewrites only a shortcut that
36	already belongs to the same agent directory; it preserves the collision protection for another
37	repo's agent with the same name.
38	
39	## Prevention
40	
41	Generated shortcuts delegate to the active boot skill and must never contain a plugin-cache path
42	or absolute `agent-boot.md` path. `/lr:check` detects the obsolete forms.
43	
44	## See Also
45	
46	- `docs/register-repo.md` — canonical registration templates.
47	- `docs/check.md` — static shortcut-format check.
48	- `docs/doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md` — different issue: stale plugin content rather than a stale
49	  shortcut path.
50	
````

=== new contract test file ===
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Static contract tests for version-independent registered boot shortcuts."""

import os
import unittest


FRAMEWORK_DIR = os.environ.get("LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR") or os.path.abspath(
    os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "lore-framework")
)


def read(relative_path):
    with open(os.path.join(FRAMEWORK_DIR, relative_path), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        return fh.read()


class ShortcutBootstrapContractTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_registration_template_has_no_boot_path_placeholder(self):
        content = read("docs/register-repo.md")
        self.assertNotIn("Read `<agent-boot-path>`", content)
        self.assertIn("<shortcut-bootstrap>", content)
        self.assertIn("Resolve the shortcut bootstrap", content)

    def test_each_engine_owns_an_active_boot_skill_binding(self):
        expected = {
            "docs/engines/claude.md": "installed `/lr:boot` skill",
            "docs/engines/cursor.md": "installed `/lr-boot` skill",
            "docs/engines/codex.md": "installed `lr:boot` skill",
        }
        for path, skill_name in expected.items():
            content = read(path)
            self.assertIn("## Registered shortcut bootstrap", content, path)
            self.assertIn(skill_name, content, path)
            self.assertIn("boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`", content, path)

    def test_doctor_and_check_reject_stale_cache_pins(self):
        doctor = read("docs/doctor.md")
        ailment = read("docs/doctor-stale-shortcut-bootstrap.md")
        check = read("docs/check.md")
        self.assertIn("doctor-stale-shortcut-bootstrap", doctor)
        self.assertIn("plugins/cache/", ailment)
        self.assertIn("plugins/cache/", check)
        self.assertIn("absolute `agent-boot.md` path", check)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

1	# Migration to Version 6
2	
3	## Summary
4	
5	Regenerates shortcut commands (`.claude/commands/lr-*-agent.md`) to use absolute paths instead of `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`. This eliminates the boot-time discovery scan and the unresolved `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` variable (which Claude Code does not resolve in custom commands).
6	
7	**Changes:**
8	1. **Regenerate shortcut commands** with absolute paths to both `agent-boot.md` and the agent directory.
9	
10	## Scope
11	
12	Apply per lore agent repo. The file modifications happen in `.claude/commands/` in the **domain directory**. The first repo to reach v6 applies it; subsequent repos re-run idempotently and find nothing to do.
13	
14	## Steps
15	
16	All steps must be idempotent — safe to re-run. Do **not** stamp the new version in `lore-repo.md` within this migration; the update process handles that after all steps succeed.
17	
18	### Step 1: Discover agents
19	
20	Scan all directories in the domain for lore agent repos (directories containing `lore-repo.md` at the root). For each repo, scan `agents/` for agent directories (containing `role.md`). Build a mapping of agent-name to absolute agent directory path.
21	
22	### Step 2: Resolve agent-boot.md path
23	
24	Determine the absolute path to `docs/agent-boot.md` within the plugin. Derive it from the path you used to read this migration file — go up one level from `migrations/` to the plugin root, then into `docs/agent-boot.md`. Call this `<agent-boot-path>`.
25	
26	### Step 3: Regenerate shortcut commands
27	
28	Scan `<domain>/.claude/commands/` for files matching `lr-*-agent.md`. For each one:
29	
30	1. Read the file content.
31	2. Determine whether it is a shortcut command (content contains the phrase `boot as agent`). If not, skip.
32	3. Extract the agent name from the filename: strip the `lr-` prefix and the `-agent.md` suffix.
33	4. Look up the agent name in the mapping from Step 1. If the agent is not found in any repo, **warn** and skip — the shortcut command points to an agent that no longer exists.
34	5. Compute the new shortcut command content — exactly this single line plus a trailing newline:
35	
36	   ```
37	   Read `<agent-boot-path>` and boot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.
38	   ```
39	
40	   Where `<agent-boot-path>` is from Step 2, `<agent-name>` is the extracted name, and `<agent-dir>` is the absolute path from the mapping.
41	
42	6. **If the on-disk content matches the new content exactly**, skip (idempotent — already migrated).
43	7. **If the on-disk content matches the known v5 template** (see below), overwrite with the new content.
44	8. **If the on-disk content diverges from both**, treat as manually edited. Follow the manual-edit handling described in `update.md` — present a three-way merge (old template to new template onto on-disk content) and proceed per the user's choice.
45	
46	Record each regenerated file for the final report.
47	
48	#### Known clean v5 template
49	
50	Exactly this single line (plus a trailing newline):
51	
52	```
53	Read `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/agent-boot.md` and boot as agent `<agent-name>`.
54	```
55	
56	A file matches when the template equals the on-disk content exactly (modulo trailing whitespace), with `<agent-name>` substituted by the kebab-case name extracted from the filename.
57	
58	#### Known clean pre-v5 templates
59	
60	A file also matches as "clean" (safely overwritable) if it matches any of these older forms:
61	
62	```
63	Read `lore-framework/docs/agent-boot.md` and boot as agent `<agent-name>`.
64	```
65	
66	This covers repos that jumped directly from v4 or earlier to v6 without going through v5 first.
67	
68	### Step 4: Report
69	
70	After all commands are processed, print a summary:
71	
72	- **Regenerated:** list of `lr-<agent-name>-agent.md` files rewritten (or "none").
73	- **Merged manually:** list of files that needed three-way merge (or "none").
74	- **Skipped (missing agent):** list of commands whose agent was not found (or "none").
75	
76	## Write Paths
77	
78	```
79	(none)
80	```
81	
82	Migration 6 writes only to workspace-root paths (`.claude/commands/lr-*-agent.md`). The boot-time collision gate is per-repo and cannot see workspace-root files (see `conventions.md` § Known gap: workspace-root paths), so a Write Paths declaration would be decorative. The three-way-merge protection for manual edits is implemented inside Step 3 itself via known-template matching.
83	
84	## Dry-Run Mode
85	
86	When invoked from `/lr:update --dry-run`, detect all the same cases but do not modify any files. Report each would-be change:
87	
88	- `would regenerate: <path>`
89	- `would prompt (manual edits): <path>`
90	- `would warn (missing agent): <path>`
91	
92	Do not prompt the user in dry-run mode.
93	
94	## Notes
95	
96	- All steps in this migration are idempotent — safe to re-run after a partial failure.
97	- `.claude/commands/` lives in the **domain directory**, shared across all lore agent repos. Running this migration against the first repo handles all shared commands; subsequent repos find nothing to do.
98	- Do not update `lore-repo.md` frontmatter in this doc. The `/lr:update` process stamps the repo version only after all migrations succeed.

## Backfill discipline

Every finalization that lands a `VERSION` bump must add the new version's entry here — including kind, scope summary, and cache-affecting annotation. Backfill at the same finalization that ships the version is the principled timing — not "we'll get to it later." Drift in the history list erodes the topic's value as a per-version classification index.

**Scan the whole tail for gaps, not just the current version — the discipline is not self-healing (v21 lesson).** Appending only the current version's entry each ship silently carries forward any gap a prior slipped ship left. Landing v21 (2026-07-06), I found the history was missing **both** v20 and v21 — the v20 ship had slipped the backfill entirely, and appending only v21 would have left the v20 gap permanent. So at every ship, before adding the new entry, scan the tail between the last recorded version and the one shipping and backfill **all** missing entries in the same ship. Cheap check: `for v in <last+1>..<current>; do grep -c "v$v " versioning-release-types.md; done` — any `0` is a gap. This is a bounded mechanical sweep, so it belongs in the current ship (`feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`). `/lr:check` has no check for this yet — a candidate: assert a history entry exists for every `release-notes/<N>.md` / `migrations/<N>.md` (see `consistency-checks.md`).

This discipline composes with the deferral-discipline rule (`feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`): the backfill is a bounded mechanical task that fits in the current ship's scope. It also composes with the cache-clear-footer convention: the same finalization should both (a) ensure the release-notes has the footer if applicable, and (b) record the cache-affecting status in this history list.

The role.md responsibilities call this out explicitly — see `role.md` § Lore-Curation Disciplines.

**"Commit pending" can accumulate across versions — verify `git HEAD` at session start.** When lore
says a version is "built, commit pending," don't assume the last-described version is the committed
one. At the start of the v18/v19 ship, the public repo `HEAD` was still at v17 — *both* v18 (lr-wait,
recorded as "commit pending") and the whole port were uncommitted in one working tree, so shipping
required untangling and committing in order (v18 first, then v19), not a single push. Ground the
current state in `git log`/`git HEAD`, not in the most recent lore entry (a `verify-before-acting`
instance — `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`).

## In-band BETA refinement (post-v10 observation)

When a BETA feature needs refinement after its initial release, the version-bump ceremony is **not** required. Pattern:

1. **Edit the procedure doc** (`docs/<feature>.md`) — source of truth for current behavior.
2. **Leave the release notes alone** (`release-notes/<N>.md`) — they're a historical record, frozen at the version they describe. A verbatim citation that goes stale post-refinement is acceptable.
3. **No `VERSION` bump** — the BETA caveat ("internal procedure may evolve based on real-world usage") in the release notes is the contract that licenses this.
4. **Update lore at finalization** — design-decisions topics for the feature reflect the *current* state, not the originally-shipped state.

When the feature graduates from BETA, the graduation release notes describe the cumulative final state, not each iteration. Stable (non-BETA) features need a different cadence — refinements there are full release-notes events with version bumps.

**When to break this pattern** (not yet observed; anticipated):

- **Breaking change within BETA** — if existing usage will fail in a non-obvious way, mark explicitly with release notes + bump.
- **New state, file format, or per-agent metadata** — needs migration tracking regardless of stability label.
- **Refinement large enough to be a new feature** — graduate the BETA or treat as v(N+1).

First observed instance: spawn-teammate post-v10 boot-prompt reframe (see `spawn-teammate-feature.md`). If more BETA refinements accumulate and the playbook stays stable, this section should be promoted to a standalone topic (`beta-refinement-workflow.md`).

## See Also

- `cache-clear-footer-convention.md` — the v12 authoring convention this topic's cache-affecting axis tracks; the manual cache-propagation lever.
- `plugin-manifest-versioning.md` — the v14 cache-propagation lever (manifest `1.<VERSION>.0` bump); the primary release-detection mechanism.
- `update-process.md` — how the update flow applies migration + release-notes artifacts.
- `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md` — discipline that the backfill rule applies to itself.
- `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md` — the v14 portability rule; the bug that made v13 auto-pull a macOS no-op.
- `dirty-tree-gates-write-vs-read-distinction.md` — v15 extends the write-side with the collision-check refinement.
- `spawn-teammate-feature.md` — v15 Step 7 disambiguation, Step 7c verification, teammate-conventions integration.
- `graduated-verification-confidence.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md` — v15-promoted foundational topics.
- `trilens-loop-feature.md` — the v30 feature; `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md` — the v30-promoted foundational topic.
- `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md` — the artifact-state discipline that keeps a ship's recorded validation honest.
```
